# Step 1: Filter customers who are 'Active' and joined on or after January 1, 2023.
# 'sbCustStatus' is used for the customer's status.
# 'sbCustJoinDate' is used for the customer's join date.
active_and_recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    (sbCustStatus == "Active") & (sbCustJoinDate >= "2023-01-01")
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered customers by their country ('sbCustCountry')
# and then calculate the count of customers for each country.
# The 'name' parameter in PARTITION gives a conceptual name to the groups.
# The 'by' parameter specifies the field to partition on, which is 'sbCustCountry'.
# In the CALCULATE step:
#   - 'country=sbCustCountry' selects the country from the partition key.
#   - 'tac_count=COUNT(Customers)' counts the number of customers within each country group.
result = active_and_recent_customers.PARTITION(
    name="country_group", by=(sbCustCountry)
).CALCULATE(
    country=sbCustCountry,
    tac_count=COUNT(Customers)
)

# Filter transactions for the last 8 weeks excluding current week
transactions_last_8_weeks = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 8)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_id=transaction_id,
    week_start=DATETIME(date_time, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=(DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 1) | (DAYOFWEEK(date_time) == 7)  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate counts
result = transactions_last_8_weeks.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_transactions=COUNT(Transactions),
    weekend_transactions=SUM(Transactions.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

# Step 1: Calculate necessary information for each transaction from the Broker's Transactions collection
transactions_with_info = Broker.Transactions.CALCULATE(
    transaction_datetime_col = date_time,
    ticker_type_col = ticker.ticker_type,  # Access ticker_type from the related Ticker
    # Calculate how many full weeks ago this transaction occurred relative to the start of the current week.
    # A value of 1 means last week, 2 means the week before last, etc.
    weeks_ago_calculated = DATEDIFF("weeks", date_time, DATETIME('now', 'start of week')),
    # Flag for weekend transactions (1 if weekend, 0 if weekday)
    is_weekend_calculated = IFF(ISIN(DAYNAME(date_time), ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
)

# Step 2: Filter for stock transactions in the last 8 weeks (excluding the current week)
filtered_relevant_transactions = transactions_with_info.WHERE(
    (ticker_type_col == 'stock') &
    (weeks_ago_calculated >= 1) &  # Excludes current week (weeks_ago_calculated = 0)
    (weeks_ago_calculated <= 8)   # Includes up to 8 weeks ago
)

# Step 3: Group by week and calculate the required counts
# The PARTITION function groups the filtered_relevant_transactions by the weeks_ago_calculated value.
# For each group (each week), we then calculate the total and weekend transaction counts.
weekly_stock_transaction_counts = filtered_relevant_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_group", by=(weeks_ago_calculated)  # Partition by the calculated 'weeks_ago' value
).CALCULATE(
    week_past_identifier = weeks_ago_calculated,  # This is the 'Nth week ago' identifier (1-8)
    total_stock_txns = COUNT(Transactions),      # Counts all stock transactions in this weekly group
    weekend_stock_txns = SUM(is_weekend_calculated) # Sums the is_weekend_calculated flag (0 or 1) for transactions in this group
).ORDER_BY(week_past_identifier.ASC())  # Order results from most recent week (1) to oldest (8)

result = weekly_stock_transaction_counts

# Define the start of the current week
start_of_current_week_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')

# Define the start of last week (7 days before the start of the current week)
start_of_last_week_val = DATETIME(start_of_current_week_val, '-7 days')

# Filter transactions based on customer's country and the date range for last week
# Transactions.customer.country accesses the country of the customer who made the transaction.
# The date_time of the transaction must be on or after the start of last week
# and before the start of the current week.
filtered_transactions_last_week_usa = Transactions.WHERE(
    (customer.country == "USA") &
    (date_time >= start_of_last_week_val) &
    (date_time < start_of_current_week_val)
)

# Calculate the total number of transactions and their total amount at the Broker level
result = Broker.CALCULATE(
    num_transactions = COUNT(filtered_transactions_last_week_usa),
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(filtered_transactions_last_week_usa.amount)
)

INFO:httpx:HTTP Request: POST https://us-east5-aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1/projects/solid-drive-448717-p8/locations/us-east5/publishers/anthropic/models/claude-sonnet-4@20250514:streamRawPredict "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"
[DEBUG] [Q8] Code generated by claude:
# Define the date range: last 6 months excluding current month
recent_customers = Customers.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        join_date,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
)

# Calculate customer info with join month
customer_info = recent_customers.CALCULATE(
    customer_id=_id,
    join_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(join_date), LPAD(MONTH(join_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Group by month to calculate PMCS (per month customer signups)
pmcs_data = customer_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=join_month).CALCULATE(
    join_month=join_month,
    pmcs=COUNT(Customers)
)

# Calculate PMAT (per month average transaction amount) for these customers
# First get transaction info for these customers in the same time period
transaction_info = recent_customers.transactions_made.WHERE(
    MONOTONIC(
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months'),
        date_time,
        DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 day')
    )
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date_time), LPAD(MONTH(date_time), 2, "0")),
    amount=amount
)

# Group transactions by month to calculate PMAT
pmat_data = transaction_info.PARTITION(name="tx_months", by=transaction_month).CALCULATE(

)

# Step 1: Define the date range for the last 6 months, excluding the current month.
# 'start_of_filter_period' will be the first day of the month, 6 months ago.
# 'end_of_filter_period' will be the first day of the current month (exclusive).
date_references = Broker.CALCULATE(
    end_of_filter_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month'),
    start_of_filter_period=DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
)

# Step 2: Filter customers who joined within this period.
# Also, extract their signup year and month for grouping.
customers_in_period = date_references.Customers.WHERE(
    (join_date >= start_of_filter_period) & (join_date < end_of_filter_period)
).CALCULATE(
    # _id is implicitly available for COUNT(Customers) later
    signup_yr=YEAR(join_date),
    signup_mth=MONTH(join_date)
    # Customer's transactions will be accessed via Customers.transactions_made in the partition step

monthly_cohort_calculations = customers_in_period.PARTITION(

).CALCULATE(
    # Pass through partition keys for the final output
    result_signup_year=signup_yr,
    result_signup_month=signup_mth,
    # PMCS: Per Month Customer Signups (count of customers in this cohort)
    calculated_pmcs=COUNT(Customers),
    # Components for PMAT:
    # Sum of all transaction amounts by customers who signed up in this specific month/year
    total_transaction_sum=SUM(Customers.transactions_made.amount),
    # Count of all transactions made by customers who signed up in this specific month/year
    total_transaction_count=COUNT(Customers.transactions_made)
)

    signup_year=result_signup_year,
    signup_month=result_signup_month,
    pmcs=calculated_pmcs,
    pmat=IFF(total_transaction_count > 0, total_transaction_sum / total_transaction_count, 0.0)
).ORDER_BY(signup_year.ASC(), signup_month.ASC())

# Step 1: Filter customers who joined in 2022
customers_joined_2022 = Customers.WHERE(YEAR(join_date) == 2022)

# Step 2: For these customers, calculate flags for having transactions and being active, and get their country
customer_details_for_ar = customers_joined_2022.CALCULATE(
    cust_country_attr = country,  # Attribute for partitioning
    has_transactions_flag = HAS(transactions_made) == 1,
    is_active_customer_flag = status == "Active" # Assuming 'Active' status string
)

# Step 3: Partition these customers by country
# Each group in the partition will contain customers from a specific country who joined in 2022
country_partitioned_data = customer_details_for_ar.PARTITION(name="country_customer_group", by=(cust_country_attr))

# Step 4: Calculate AR for each country
# Within each country_customer_group:
# - Numerator: Count customers who are active AND have transactions.
# - Denominator: Count customers who have transactions.
ar_calculation_by_country = country_partitioned_data.CALCULATE(
    country_output_name = cust_country_attr,
    # Count active customers with transactions within this country group
    num_active_with_transactions = COUNT(country_customer_group.Customers.WHERE(is_active_customer_flag == 1 & has_transactions_flag == 1)),
    # Count total customers with transactions within this country group
    total_customers_with_transactions = COUNT(country_customer_group.Customers.WHERE(has_transactions_flag == 1)),
    # Calculate Activity Ratio, handling division by zero
    activity_ratio_value = IFF(
        total_customers_with_transactions > 0,
        (100.0 * num_active_with_transactions) / total_customers_with_transactions,
        0.0  # AR is 0 if no customers with transactions
    )
)

# Step 5: Select the final required fields: country and AR
result = ar_calculation_by_country.CALCULATE(country=country_output_name, AR=activity_ratio_value)

# Define the start date for the "past month, inclusive of 1 month ago"
# This means from the start of the day, one month prior to today, up to the current time.
start_date_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of day', '-1 month')

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only 'sell' transactions within the defined date range.
# We start from Broker.Transactions as Transactions is a top-level collection under Broker.
filtered_sell_transactions = Broker.Transactions.WHERE(
    (transaction_type == "sell") & (date_time >= start_date_val)
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered sell transactions by ticker symbol.
# For each ticker symbol, calculate the sum of amount, tax, and commission.
# 'ticker.symbol' is accessible because Transactions has a 'ticker' relationship to the Tickers collection, which has a 'symbol' field.
# Inside CALCULATE, 'Transactions.amount', 'Transactions.tax', and 'Transactions.commission' refer to the
# transactions within the current partition group (i.e., for a specific ticker_symbol).
aggregated_data_by_ticker = filtered_sell_transactions.PARTITION(name="sells_by_ticker", by=(ticker.symbol)).CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_value = ticker.symbol,  # The partition key
    total_sell_amount = SUM(Transactions.amount),
    total_sell_tax = SUM(Transactions.tax),
    total_sell_commission = SUM(Transactions.commission)
)

# Step 3: Calculate the Selling Profit Margin (SPM) for each ticker symbol.
# The SPM formula is (Total Amount from Sells - (Tax + Commission)) / Total Amount from Sells * 100.
# We use IFF to handle cases where total_sell_amount might be zero to prevent division by zero errors.
spm_calculated_per_ticker = aggregated_data_by_ticker.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol_output = ticker_symbol_value,
    calculated_spm = IFF(
        total_sell_amount == 0,
        0.0,  # If total sell amount is 0, SPM is 0.0 (or None, if preferred)
        (total_sell_amount - (total_sell_tax + total_sell_commission)) * 100.0 / total_sell_amount
    )
)

# Step 4: Select the final required fields: ticker symbol and SPM.
result = spm_calculated_per_ticker.CALCULATE(
    ticker_symbol = ticker_symbol_output,
    SPM = calculated_spm
)

    ticker_symbol=ticker.symbol,
    month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(date), LPAD(MONTH(date), 2, "0"))

)

    name="transaction_types", 
    by=transaction_type
).CALCULATE(

    distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id),

# Step 1: Filter transactions to the specified date range.
# We compare the date part of 'date_time' by truncating it to the 'start of day'.
filtered_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') >= DATETIME('2023-01-01', 'start of day')) &
    (DATETIME(date_time, 'start of day') <= DATETIME('2023-03-31', 'start of day'))
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by 'transaction_type'.
# For each type, calculate the number of distinct customers and the average number of shares.
transaction_type_summary = filtered_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="txn_type_groups", by=(transaction_type)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,  # The partition key
    num_distinct_customers=NDISTINCT(Transactions.customer_id), # Count distinct customers in this group
    avg_num_shares=AVG(Transactions.shares)  # Calculate average shares for this group
)

# Step 3: Select the top 3 transaction types by the number of distinct customers.
# Then, select the required fields for the final output.
result = transaction_type_summary.TOP_K(
    3, by=num_distinct_customers.DESC()
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_type=transaction_type,
    num_distinct_customers=num_distinct_customers,
    avg_shares=avg_num_shares # Renaming for the final output as "average number of shares"
)

# Then, for each combination, count the number of transactions

# Filter transactions from the past 30 days (inclusive of 30 days ago)
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30
)

transactions_with_country = recent_transactions.CALCULATE(
    country=customer.country,
    amount=amount
)

)

[DEBUG] [Q26] Code generated by gemini:
# Filter transactions to the last 30 days (inclusive)
recent_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("days", date_time, DATETIME('now')) <= 30) & (date_time <= DATETIME('now'))
)

# Add customer country and transaction amount to the filtered transactions
# customer.country gives the country of the customer who made the transaction
transactions_with_country_details = recent_transactions.CALCULATE(
    cust_country = customer.country,
    transaction_value = amount
)

# Partition by customer country
country_grouped_transactions = transactions_with_country_details.PARTITION(
    name="country_transactions_group",  # Name for the partition group
    by=(cust_country)
)

# Calculate aggregates for each country: number of transactions and total transaction amount
country_aggregates = country_grouped_transactions.CALCULATE(
    country_name = cust_country,  # The country name is the partition key
    num_of_transactions = COUNT(transactions_with_country_details),  # Count transactions within this country's partition
    total_transaction_amount = SUM(transactions_with_country_details.transaction_value)  # Sum transaction amounts within this country's partition
)

# Get the top 5 countries by total transaction amount
top_countries_by_transaction_amount = country_aggregates.TOP_K(
    5,
    by=total_transaction_amount.DESC()
)

result = top_countries_by_transaction_amount

    (MONTH(date_time) <= 3) &

# Get the date range for last 8 weeks (excluding current week)
date_info = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-0 weeks'),
    eight_weeks_ago=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-9 weeks')
)

# Get payments from high-value sales in the target date range
high_value_payments = date_info.Sales.WHERE(sale_price > 30000).payment.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= eight_weeks_ago) & 
    (payment_date < current_week_start)
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(payment_date), (1, 7))
)

# Group by week and calculate totals
result = high_value_payments.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week=week_start,
    total_payments=COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q32] Code generated by gemini:
result = Dealership.CALCULATE(
    # Define date boundaries: the start of the current week and the start of the week 8 weeks ago.
    # These values will be available downstream for filtering.
    current_week_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    eight_weeks_ago_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-8 weeks')
).PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    # Filter payments to include only those associated with sales over $30,000.
    # sale_record links PaymentsReceived to Sales, and sale_price is a field in Sales.
    sale_record.sale_price > 30000
).CALCULATE(
    # For each filtered payment, calculate the start date of the week it falls into
    # and determine if the payment was made on a weekend.
    # payment_date is a field in PaymentsReceived.
    # DAYNAME returns the name of the day (e.g., "Saturday").
    week_start_for_payment = DATETIME(payment_date, 'start of week'),
    is_payment_on_weekend = ISIN(DAYNAME(payment_date), ("Saturday", "Sunday"))
    # current_week_start_val and eight_weeks_ago_start_val are implicitly carried forward.
).WHERE(
    # Filter these payments to include only those from the last 8 full calendar weeks,
    # excluding the current week.
    (week_start_for_payment >= eight_weeks_ago_start_val) & (week_start_for_payment < current_week_start_val)
).PARTITION(
    # Group the remaining payments by the start date of their respective weeks.
    name="weekly_group", by=(week_start_for_payment)
).CALCULATE(
    # For each week, calculate the required outputs:
    # - week: The start date of the week (this is the partition key).
    # - total_payments_received: The total count of payments in that week.
    #   COUNT(PaymentsReceived) counts the records from the PaymentsReceived collection
    #   that fall into the current partition group after all preceding filters.
    # - weekend_payments_received: The count of payments made on a weekend in that week.
    #   SUM(IFF(is_payment_on_weekend, 1, 0)) sums up 1 for each payment where
    #   the pre-calculated is_payment_on_weekend field is true.
    week = week_start_for_payment,
    total_payments_received = COUNT(PaymentsReceived),
    weekend_payments_received = SUM(IFF(is_payment_on_weekend, 1, 0))
).ORDER_BY(week.ASC()) # Order the final results by week in ascending order.

# The LIKE function with UPPER ensures case-insensitivity and partial matching.

    car_model=model,     # Selects the 'model' from the partition key
    num_sales=COUNT(Cars.sale_records)  # Counts the number of sales records for cars within the current make/model group

# Step 1: Calculate aggregated sales data for each salesperson.
# This includes their first name, last name, the sum of their sales (total_sales_amount),
# and the count of sales they made (number_of_sales).
sales_data_per_salesperson = Salespersons.CALCULATE(
    sp_fn=first_name,  # Temporary alias for first_name
    sp_ln=last_name,   # Temporary alias for last_name
    agg_total_sales=SUM(sales_made.sale_price),  # Sum of sale_price from all sales_made by the salesperson
    agg_num_sales=COUNT(sales_made)              # Count of sales_made by the salesperson
)

# Step 2: Calculate the sales rank (SR) and format the final output.
# The SR is determined by ranking salespersons based on their agg_total_sales in descending order.
# The result is then ordered by SR in ascending order to show the top salesperson first.
final_result = sales_data_per_salesperson.CALCULATE(
    first_name=sp_fn,                        # Final field: first_name
    last_name=sp_ln,                         # Final field: last_name
    total_sales_amount=agg_total_sales,      # Final field: total_sales_amount
    number_of_sales=agg_num_sales,           # Final field: number_of_sales
    SR=RANKING(by=agg_total_sales.DESC())    # Calculate Sales Rank (SR)
).ORDER_BY(SR.ASC())                         # Order the results by SR (ascending)

cars_with_latest_inventory = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,

# Step 1: Start from the Sales collection. For each sale, retrieve information about the car,
# its make, model, and determine its latest inventory status.
sales_with_car_details = Sales.CALCULATE(
    # Store the sale price for later aggregation.
    sale_price_val=sale_price,
    # Get the make and model of the car involved in the sale.
    car_make_val=car.make,
    car_model_val=car.model,
    # Determine if the car is no longer in inventory based on its latest snapshot.
    # Access the car's inventory_snapshots.
    # RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1 finds the latest snapshot(s) for this specific car.
    # .SINGULAR() selects one if multiple (e.g., same date, though is_in_inventory should be consistent).
    # Check if its is_in_inventory status is False.
    is_car_no_longer_in_inventory=(car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=snapshot_date.DESC(), allow_ties=True) == 1
    ).SINGULAR().is_in_inventory == False)
)

# Step 2: Filter these sales records to include only those where the car
# is confirmed to be no longer in inventory.
# Cars in the Sales collection are inherently "sold".
filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory = sales_with_car_details.WHERE(
    sales_with_car_details.is_car_no_longer_in_inventory == True
)

# Step 3: Group these filtered sales by car make and model.
# For each group, calculate the maximum sale price.
# The collection being partitioned is `filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory`.
# `MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)` will compute the max sale_price_val
# from the sales records within each (make, model) group.
max_sale_prices_by_make_model = filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.PARTITION(
    name="make_model_group", by=(car_make_val, car_model_val)
).CALCULATE(
    # Output make and model from the partition keys.
    output_make=car_make_val,
    output_model=car_model_val,
    # Calculate the highest sale price for this group.
    output_highest_sale_price=MAX(filtered_sales_for_cars_not_in_inventory.sale_price_val)
)

# Step 4: Select the final required fields (make, model, highest_sale_price)
# and order the results by the highest sale price in descending order.
result = max_sale_prices_by_make_model.CALCULATE(
    make=output_make,
    model=output_model,
    highest_sale_price=output_highest_sale_price
).ORDER_BY(highest_sale_price.DESC())

# Filter salespersons hired between 2022 and 2023
eligible_salespersons = Salespersons.WHERE(
    (YEAR(hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(hire_date) <= 2023)
)

# Get sales from eligible salespersons in the last 6 months excluding current month
sales_info = eligible_salespersons.sales_made.WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", sale_date, DATETIME('now')) <= 6)
).CALCULATE(
    sale_month=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month')
)

# Group by month and calculate metrics
monthly_metrics = sales_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=sale_month).CALCULATE(
    month=sale_month,
    PMSPS=COUNT(Sales),
    PMSR=SUM(Sales.sale_price)
)

# To ensure all 6 months are included (even with 0 values), we need to create a complete month range
# and left join with our metrics
result = monthly_metrics.ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Define the start and end of the calculation period (last 6 months, excluding current month)
calc_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-6 months')
calc_end_date_exclusive = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Prepare sales data with necessary attributes:
# - attr_sale_month: The month of the sale (e.g., '2023-11-01')
# - attr_is_target_salesperson: Boolean indicating if the salesperson meets the hire date criteria
# - attr_sale_price: The price of the sale
sales_prepared = Sales.CALCULATE(
    attr_sale_month = DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of month'),
    attr_is_target_salesperson = (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) >= 2022) & (YEAR(salesperson.hire_date) <= 2023),
    attr_sale_price = sale_price
)

# Filter these sales to include only those within the defined 6-month period
sales_for_period = sales_prepared.WHERE(
    (attr_sale_month >= calc_start_date) & (attr_sale_month < calc_end_date_exclusive)
)

# Partition the filtered sales by month.
# This will create groups for each month that has at least one sale in sales_for_period.
# If a month within the 6-month window has zero sales overall from any salesperson, it won't appear.
# If it has sales, but none from target salespersons, it will appear with PMSPS=0 and PMSR=0 for those target salespersons.
monthly_performance = sales_for_period.PARTITION(name="sales_per_month", by=(attr_sale_month)).CALCULATE(
    month = attr_sale_month,
    # PMSPS: Count sales within this month's group that were made by a target salesperson
    PMSPS = COUNT(sales_per_month.Sales.WHERE(attr_is_target_salesperson == True)),
    # PMSR: Sum sale prices within this month's group for sales made by a target salesperson
    # Default to 0.0 if there are no such sales (SUM of empty set is typically 0, but explicit default is safer)
    PMSR = DEFAULT_TO(SUM(sales_per_month.Sales.WHERE(attr_is_target_salesperson == True).attr_sale_price), 0.0)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())

# Find cars that were sold on the same day they went out of inventory
cars_sold_same_day = Cars.WHERE(
    HAS(sale_records) == 1 & HAS(inventory_snapshots) == 1
).CALCULATE(
    car_make=make,
    car_model=model,
    # Find the last inventory date when the car was in inventory
    last_inventory_date=MAX(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == True).snapshot_date),
    # Get sale information for sales that match the last inventory date
    matching_sales=sale_records.WHERE(sale_date == MAX(inventory_snapshots.WHERE(is_in_inventory == True).snapshot_date))
).WHERE(
    HAS(matching_sales) == 1
).CALCULATE(

)

[DEBUG] [Q43] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter sales to find those where the car was sold on the same day it went out of inventory.
# A car "went out of inventory" is indicated by an InventorySnapshots record
# for that car where 'is_in_inventory' is False.
# The condition checks if there's at least one such snapshot whose 'snapshot_date'
# matches the 'sale_date' of the sale.
# 'sale_date' in the inner WHERE refers to the 'sale_date' of the current 'Sales' record being processed.
# 'car.inventory_snapshots' accesses the related inventory snapshots for the car involved in the sale.
sales_meeting_condition = Sales.WHERE(
    HAS(
        car.inventory_snapshots.WHERE(
            (snapshot_date == sale_date) & (is_in_inventory == False)
        )
    ) == 1
)

# Step 2: From these filtered sales, select the one with the highest 'sale_price'.
# TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC()) achieves this by ordering sales by 'sale_price'
# in descending order and picking the first one. If multiple sales share the same highest price,
# one will be chosen.
highest_priced_sale_record = sales_meeting_condition.TOP_K(1, by=sale_price.DESC())

# Step 3: From the selected sale record, extract the car's make, model, and the sale price.
# 'car.make' and 'car.model' access attributes from the related 'Cars' record via the 'car' relationship.
# 'sale_price' is an attribute of the 'Sales' record itself.
result = highest_priced_sale_record.CALCULATE(
    make=car.make,
    model=car.model,
    sale_price=sale_price
)

# Step 1: Extract month/year info and group payments by month
payment_info = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    year=YEAR(payment_date),
    month=MONTH(payment_date),
    year_month=JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(payment_date), LPAD(MONTH(payment_date), 2, "0"))
)

# Step 2: Group by year-month and calculate total payments per month
monthly_payments = payment_info.PARTITION(name="months", by=(year, month)).CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

result = monthly_payments.CALCULATE(
    year=year,
    month=month,
    year_month=year_month,
    total_payments=total_payments,
    prev_month_payments=PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0),
    mom_change=total_payments - PREV(total_payments, by=(year.ASC(), month.ASC()), default=0)
).ORDER_BY(year.ASC(), month.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q44] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Add year and month columns to PaymentsReceived data
payments_with_year_month = PaymentsReceived.CALCULATE(
    pay_year=YEAR(payment_date),
    pay_month=MONTH(payment_date)
    # payment_amount is an original field and will be available
)

# Step 2: Group by year and month, then calculate the sum of payments for each group.
# Order the results chronologically for the PREV function.
monthly_aggregated_payments = payments_with_year_month.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_payment_group", by=(pay_year, pay_month)
).CALCULATE(
    pay_year=pay_year,  # This is the partition key
    pay_month=pay_month, # This is the partition key
    total_monthly_payment_amount=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) # Sum amounts from original table for the current group
).ORDER_BY(pay_year.ASC(), pay_month.ASC())

# Step 3: Calculate the Month-over-Month (MoM) change.
# The PREV function looks at the previous record in the ordered dataset.
result = monthly_aggregated_payments.CALCULATE(
    payment_year=pay_year,
    payment_month=pay_month,
    total_payments_for_month=total_monthly_payment_amount,
    # MoM change = current month's total - previous month's total.
    # PREV(total_monthly_payment_amount, by=(...)) retrieves the total_monthly_payment_amount
    # from the previous row based on the specified ordering.
    # If there's no previous row (i.e., for the first month), PREV returns None,
    # and (value - None) results in None, which is appropriate.
    month_over_month_change=total_monthly_payment_amount - PREV(total_monthly_payment_amount, by=(pay_year.ASC(), pay_month.ASC()))
)

[DEBUG] [Q53] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Augment Sales data with customer's state, the sale price, and customer's ID.
# - customer_state_field: The state of the customer associated with the sale.
# - current_sale_price: The sale price of the transaction.
# - customer_identifier: The unique ID of the customer.
sales_details = Sales.CALCULATE(
    customer_state_field = customer.state,
    current_sale_price = sale_price,
    customer_identifier = customer._id
)

# Step 2: Partition the augmented sales data by customer_state_field.
# This creates groups of sales records, where each group corresponds to a unique customer state.
state_grouped_sales = sales_details.PARTITION(name="state_sales_group", by=(customer_state_field))

# Step 3: For each state group, calculate the state name, total revenue, and number of unique customers.
# - state_name: The customer state for the current group.
# - total_revenue_val: Sum of 'current_sale_price' from the 'sales_details' collection for sales in this state group.
# - num_unique_customers_val: Count of distinct 'customer_identifier' from the 'sales_details' collection for sales in this state group.
state_revenue_summary = state_grouped_sales.CALCULATE(
    state_name = customer_state_field,
    total_revenue_val = SUM(sales_details.current_sale_price),
    num_unique_customers_val = NDISTINCT(sales_details.customer_identifier)
)

# Step 4: Select the top 5 states based on total_revenue_val, in descending order.
# The result will include state_name, total_revenue_val, and num_unique_customers_val for these top states.
result = state_revenue_summary.TOP_K(5, by=total_revenue_val.DESC())

# Filter payments from the previous ISO week and group by date and payment method
previous_week_payments = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    # Get payments from 14 days ago to 7 days ago (previous ISO week)
    (DATEDIFF("days", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) >= 7) &
    (DATEDIFF("days", payment_date, DATETIME("now")) < 14) &
    # Ensure we're getting the correct ISO week by checking day of week alignment
    (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) >= DAYOFWEEK(DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-7 days"))) &
    (DAYOFWEEK(payment_date) <= DAYOFWEEK(DATETIME("now", "start of week", "-1 day")))
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date_only = payment_date,
    payment_method_type = payment_method,
    amount = payment_amount
)

# Group by date and payment method to get daily totals
result = previous_week_payments.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_groups", 
    by=(payment_date_only, payment_method_type)
).CALCULATE(
    payment_date = payment_date_only,
    payment_method = payment_method_type,
    daily_total = SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())

# Define the start and end dates for the previous ISO week.
# Assumes DATETIME('now', 'start of week') correctly identifies Monday as the start of an ISO week.
start_of_current_week_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of week')
start_of_previous_week_dt = DATETIME(start_of_current_week_dt, '-7 days')
end_of_previous_week_dt = DATETIME(start_of_current_week_dt, '-1 day') # This will be Sunday of the previous week

# Filter payments that occurred within the calculated previous ISO week.
payments_in_previous_week = PaymentsReceived.WHERE(
    (payment_date >= start_of_previous_week_dt) & (payment_date <= end_of_previous_week_dt)
)

# Group the filtered payments by payment_date and payment_method.
# Then, calculate the sum of payment_amount for each group.
# The fields 'payment_date' and 'payment_method' from the PARTITION's 'by' clause
# are used to define 'calc_payment_date' and 'calc_payment_method'.
# SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount) aggregates amounts for records within each partition group.
daily_payments_summary = payments_in_previous_week.PARTITION(
    name="daily_method_group", by=(payment_date, payment_method)
).CALCULATE(
    calc_payment_date=payment_date,
    calc_payment_method=payment_method,
    calc_total_amount=SUM(PaymentsReceived.payment_amount)
)

# Select the final fields for the output and order the results.
# The field names are chosen to match common expectations for such a report.
result = daily_payments_summary.CALCULATE(
    payment_date=calc_payment_date,
    payment_method=calc_payment_method,
    total_amount=calc_total_amount
).ORDER_BY(payment_date.ASC(), payment_method.ASC())

# Step 1: Filter sales for 2023 and calculate initial necessary fields.
sales_filtered = Sales.WHERE(YEAR(sale_date) == 2023).CALCULATE(
    c_state=customer.state,
    s_price_val=sale_price,
    s_date_month=MONTH(sale_date),
    s_date_year_start=DATETIME(sale_date, 'start of year') # First day of the year of the sale
)

# Step 2: Calculate the quarter start date using the fields from the previous step.
sales_info = sales_filtered.CALCULATE(
    c_state=c_state, # Pass through customer state
    s_price_val=s_price_val, # Pass through sale price
    # Determine the first date of the quarter based on the sale month
    q_start_date=IFF(
        s_date_month <= 3, s_date_year_start, # Q1
        IFF(
            s_date_month <= 6, DATETIME(s_date_year_start, '+3 months'), # Q2
            IFF(
                s_date_month <= 9, DATETIME(s_date_year_start, '+6 months'), # Q3
                DATETIME(s_date_year_start, '+9 months') # Q4
            )
        )
    )
)

# Step 3: Partition by customer state and quarter start date, then calculate total sales for each group.
quarterly_sales_by_state_result = sales_info.PARTITION(
    name="state_quarter_group",  # Name for the partition context
    by=(c_state, q_start_date)   # Partition keys: customer state and quarter start date
).CALCULATE(
    customer_state=c_state,      # Output field: customer's state (from partition key)
    quarter_start_date=q_start_date, # Output field: quarter start date (from partition key)
    total_sales=SUM(sales_info.s_price_val) # Output field: sum of sale prices for the group
)

# Assign the final result to the 'result' variable.
result = quarterly_sales_by_state_result

[DEBUG] [Q63] Code generated by gemini:
result = Drugs.CALCULATE(
    drug_name=drug_name,  # Select the drug name
    average_ddd=AVG(      # Calculate the average DDD for this drug
        # Access treatments where this drug was used
        treatments_used_in
        # Filter for treatments that have a valid end_date and where end_date is not before start_date
        .WHERE((HAS(end_dt)==1) & (end_dt >= start_dt))
        .CALCULATE(
            # Calculate the duration of the treatment in days.
            # "total days of treatment" is interpreted as inclusive of start and end days.
            # e.g., start_dt=Jan 1, end_dt=Jan 1 -> DATEDIFF is 0, so 0+1=1 day.
            # e.g., start_dt=Jan 1, end_dt=Jan 2 -> DATEDIFF is 1, so 1+1=2 days.
            # This also ensures duration is at least 1, preventing division by zero.
            treatment_duration_days_calc = DATEDIFF("days", start_dt, end_dt) + 1,

            # Calculate DDD for this specific treatment: total drug amount / total days of treatment
            ddd_value_calc = tot_drug_amt / treatment_duration_days_calc
        ).ddd_value_calc  # Select the calculated DDD value for this treatment to be averaged
    )
)

# Define date boundaries for the last 3 full months
current_month_start_dt = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
# The period of interest starts 3 months before the current month's start
# and ends just before the current month's start.
three_months_ago_start_dt = DATETIME(current_month_start_dt, '-3 months')

# Prepare treatment data: filter for the relevant period, extract month-year, and flag biologic treatments
treatments_with_info = DermTreatment.Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_start_date = start_dt, # Keep original start_dt for filtering
    # Create a sortable year-month string like "YYYY-MM"
    treatment_year_month_str = JOIN_STRINGS("-", YEAR(start_dt), LPAD(MONTH(start_dt), 2, "0")),
    patient_identifier = patient_id, # Use an alias for patient_id
    # Flag if the treatment used a biologic drug
    is_biologic_drug_treatment = (drug.drug_type == 'biologic')
).WHERE(
    # Filter treatments to be within the last three full months
    (treatment_start_date >= three_months_ago_start_dt) & (treatment_start_date < current_month_start_dt)
)

# Partition by month-year and calculate distinct patient counts
result = treatments_with_info.PARTITION(
    name="treatments_by_month", by=(treatment_year_month_str) # Group treatments by the year-month string
).CALCULATE(
    month_period = treatment_year_month_str, # Output the month period
    # Count distinct patients who had any treatment in this month
    total_distinct_patients_in_month = NDISTINCT(treatments_by_month.patient_identifier),
    # Count distinct patients who had a biologic drug treatment in this month
    biologic_distinct_patients_in_month = NDISTINCT(

# Get treatments from the last 12 months (excluding current month)
treatment_info = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month')
).WHERE(
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", treatment_month, DATETIME('now', 'start of month')) <= 12)
)

    month = treatment_month,
    PMPD = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),

# Calculate the start of the current month and the start of the month 12 months prior.
# These define the boundaries for our 12-month analysis window, excluding the current month.
current_month_start_val = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
twelve_months_ago_start_val = DATETIME(current_month_start_val, '-12 months')

# Process the Treatments collection.
# 1. For each treatment, derive 'treatment_month_year_val' by truncating 'start_dt' to the start of its month.
# 2. Filter treatments to include only those within the last 12 full months (i.e., >= 12 months ago and < current month).
treatments_in_period = Treatments.CALCULATE(
    treatment_month_year_val = DATETIME(start_dt, 'start of month')
).WHERE(
    (treatment_month_year_val >= twelve_months_ago_start_val) &
    (treatment_month_year_val < current_month_start_val)
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by 'treatment_month_year_val' to group them by month.
# Then, for each month, calculate PMPD and PMTC.
# PMPD: Number of distinct patients who received a diagnosis (via a treatment) in that month.
# PMTC: Total number of treatments administered in that month.
# The results are ordered by month in ascending order.
monthly_diagnoses_and_treatments = treatments_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_group", by=(treatment_month_year_val)
).CALCULATE(
    month_period = treatment_month_year_val,
    PMPD = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient_id),
    PMTC = COUNT(Treatments)
).ORDER_BY(month_period.ASC())

    patient_id=patient_id,
    first_treatment_year=YEAR(MIN(treatments_received.start_dt))
)

[DEBUG] [Q74] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: For each patient, determine the year of their first treatment.
# A patient's first treatment is the one with the earliest start_dt.
# We partition treatments by patient_id, find the minimum start_dt, and then extract its year.
patient_first_treatment_data = Treatments.PARTITION(
    name="treatments_by_patient", by=(patient_id)  # Group treatments by each patient
).CALCULATE(
    p_id_val = patient_id,  # Keep the patient_id
    first_treatment_start_date = MIN(Treatments.start_dt)  # Find the earliest treatment start date for this patient
).CALCULATE(
    p_id_val = p_id_val,  # Pass through patient_id
    year_of_first_treatment = YEAR(first_treatment_start_date)  # Extract the year from the earliest treatment date
)
# patient_first_treatment_data now contains: p_id_val, year_of_first_treatment for each patient.

# Step 2: Count the number of new patients for each year.
# New patients in a given year are those whose 'year_of_first_treatment' falls in that year.
# We partition the above result by 'year_of_first_treatment' and count the patients.
new_patients_counts_by_year = patient_first_treatment_data.PARTITION(
    name="patients_by_year", by=(year_of_first_treatment)  # Group patients by their first treatment year
).CALCULATE(
    current_year = year_of_first_treatment,  # The year being grouped
    num_new_patients_for_year = COUNT(patient_first_treatment_data)  # Count of patients first treated in this year
).ORDER_BY(current_year.ASC())  # Order by year, necessary for the PREV function in the next step

# Step 3: Calculate the New Patients Increase (NPI) compared to the previous year.
# NPI = (New Patients This Year) - (New Patients Previous Year)
npi_calculation = new_patients_counts_by_year.CALCULATE(
    current_year_val = current_year,
    num_new_patients_val = num_new_patients_for_year,
    # Use PREV to get the number of new patients from the previous year's record.
    # Default to 0 if there's no previous year (e.g., for the first year in the data).
    prev_year_num_new_patients_val = PREV(num_new_patients_for_year, by=current_year.ASC(), default=0)
).CALCULATE(
    year = current_year_val,  # Final field name for the year
    number_of_new_patients = num_new_patients_val,  # Final field name for the count of new patients
    NPINPI = num_new_patients_val - prev_year_num_new_patients_val  # Calculate the increase/decrease
)

result = npi_calculation

# Step 1: Calculate the global average PASI scores for day 7 and day 100.
# This calculation is performed on the 'Outcomes' collection, filtered to include
# only records where both 'day7_pasi_score' and 'day100_pasi_score' are not null.
pasi_averages = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    avg_d7_score = AVG(Outcomes.WHERE((day7_pasi_score != None) & (day100_pasi_score != None)).day7_pasi_score),
    avg_d100_score = AVG(Outcomes.WHERE((day7_pasi_score != None) & (day100_pasi_score != None)).day100_pasi_score)
)

# Step 2: Use the calculated averages to compute the D7D100PIR.
# The result will be a single row with the D7D100PIR percentage.
# Multiplying by 100.0 ensures the result is a float representing a percentage.
result = pasi_averages.CALCULATE(
    d7d100pir_percentage = (avg_d100_score - avg_d7_score) / avg_d7_score * 100.0
)

# Step 1: Filter treatments that ended in 2022 and extract the insurance type from the associated patient.
# We create an intermediate field 'treatment_ins_type' for partitioning.
treatments_for_partition = DermTreatment.Treatments.WHERE(YEAR(end_dt) == 2022).CALCULATE(
    treatment_ins_type = patient.ins_type
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered treatments by insurance type.
# For each insurance type, calculate:
#   - The average day 100 PASI score. This is done by averaging 'day100_pasi_score'
#     from the 'outcome_records' of the treatments within each insurance type group.
#   - The number of distinct patients who received these treatments. This is done by
#     counting distinct 'patient_id' from the 'patient' associated with treatments in each group.
insurance_type_summary = treatments_for_partition.PARTITION(name="ins_type_groups", by=(treatment_ins_type)).CALCULATE(
    insurance_type = treatment_ins_type,
    average_pasi_score_day_100 = AVG(Treatments.outcome_records.day100_pasi_score),
    num_distinct_patients = NDISTINCT(Treatments.patient.patient_id)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 5 insurance types.
# The sorting is by the calculated 'average_pasi_score_day_100' in ascending order (lowest first).
final_result = insurance_type_summary.TOP_K(5, by=average_pasi_score_day_100.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q86] Code generated by gemini:
# Calculate the start date for the "past 6 calendar months" window.
# This includes the current partial month and the 5 preceding full calendar months.
# For example, if 'now' is July 15th, '-5 months' is February 15th,
# and 'start of month' makes it February 1st.
date_calc = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    cutoff_date_val = DATETIME('now', '-5 months', 'start of month')
)

# Filter treatments that started on or after the cutoff_date_val.
# For these treatments, also retrieve the doctor's specialty and the total drug amount.
recent_treatments_info = date_calc.Treatments.WHERE(start_dt >= cutoff_date_val).CALCULATE(
    doc_spec = doctor.speciality, # Get the specialty from the related doctor
    treatment_drug_amt = tot_drug_amt # Get the total drug amount for the treatment
)

# Partition the filtered treatments by the doctor's specialty (doc_spec).
# Then, for each specialty, calculate the number of treatments and the sum of drug amounts.
# COUNT(Treatments) and SUM(Treatments.tot_drug_amt) refer to the Treatments
# collection implicitly filtered by the partition key (doc_spec) and the upstream WHERE clause.
specialty_aggregation = recent_treatments_info.PARTITION(name="spec_group", by=(doc_spec)).CALCULATE(
    specialty_name_calc = doc_spec,
    num_treatments_calc = COUNT(Treatments),
    total_drug_amount_calc = SUM(Treatments.tot_drug_amt)
)

# Select the top 3 specialties based on the total drug amount, in descending order.
top_3_data = specialty_aggregation.TOP_K(3, by=total_drug_amount_calc.DESC())

# Format the final result with the requested column names.
result = top_3_data.CALCULATE(
    specialty = specialty_name_calc,
    number_of_treatments = num_treatments_calc,
    total_drug_amount = total_drug_amount_calc
)

# Calculate the target registration year (current year - 2)
# This value will be available to subsequent operations on the Doctors collection.
target_year_calculation = DermTreatment.CALCULATE(
    target_registration_year=YEAR(DATETIME('now')) - 2
)

# Filter doctors by the target registration year and ensure they have treatments.
# Then, for each of these doctors, find their first treatment.
doctors_filtered_and_first_treatment_info = target_year_calculation.Doctors.WHERE(
    (year_reg == target_registration_year) & (HAS(prescribed_treatments) == 1)
).CALCULATE(
    # Select doctor's last name and year of registration
    doc_last_name=last_name,
    doc_year_of_registration=year_reg,
    # Identify the first treatment record for the current doctor.
    # RANKING is used to find the treatment(s) with the earliest start_dt.
    # per="Doctors" ensures ranking is done independently for each doctor.
    # allow_ties=True handles cases where multiple treatments might share the same earliest start date.
    # .SINGULAR() is used to pick one record if multiple treatments have the same rank 1 (earliest date).
    first_treatment_record=prescribed_treatments.WHERE(
        RANKING(by=start_dt.ASC(), per="Doctors", allow_ties=True) == 1
    ).SINGULAR()
)

# Extract the specific details from the first treatment record.
# This second CALCULATE is needed to access properties of 'first_treatment_record' defined in the previous CALCULATE.
result = doctors_filtered_and_first_treatment_info.CALCULATE(
    doctor_last_name=doc_last_name,
    doctor_year_of_registration=doc_year_of_registration,
    first_treatment_id=first_treatment_record.treatment_id,
    first_treatment_date=first_treatment_record.start_dt
)

INFO:httpx:HTTP Request: POST https://us-east5-aiplatform.googleapis.com/v1/projects/solid-drive-448717-p8/locations/us-east5/publishers/anthropic/models/claude-sonnet-4@20250514:streamRawPredict "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"
[DEBUG] [Q94] Code generated by claude:
# Get all successful transactions for each merchant (both sent and received)
merchant_transaction_info = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    # Count distinct successful transactions (both sent and received)
    total_successful_transactions=NDISTINCT(
        transactions_sent.WHERE(status == "SUCCESS").txid
    ) + NDISTINCT(
        transactions_received.WHERE(status == "SUCCESS").txid
    ),
    # Count distinct coupons used in successful transactions (both sent and received)
    distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(
        transactions_sent.WHERE((status == "SUCCESS") & (coupon_id != None)).coupon_id
    ) + NDISTINCT(
        transactions_received.WHERE((status == "SUCCESS") & (coupon_id != None)).coupon_id
    )
).WHERE(total_successful_transactions > 0)

# Calculate CPUR for each merchant
result = merchant_transaction_info.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    cpur=distinct_coupons_used / total_successful_transactions
)

merchant_cpur = Merchants.CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=name,
    num_distinct_coupons_used=NDISTINCT(transactions_received.WHERE(status == "completed").coupon_id),
    num_successful_transactions=COUNT(transactions_received.WHERE(status == "completed"))
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_name=merchant_name,
    cpur=IFF(
        num_successful_transactions == 0,
        0.0,
        num_distinct_coupons_used / num_successful_transactions
    )
)

# Filter notifications for US and Canada users from last 3 weeks (excluding current week)
us_canada_notifications = Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ("US", "Canada"))
).notifications.WHERE(
    DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME("now")) >= 1 &
    DATEDIFF("weeks", created_at, DATETIME("now")) <= 3
).CALCULATE(
    week_start=DATETIME(created_at, "start of week"),
    is_weekend=ISIN(DAYOFWEEK(created_at), (1, 7))  # Saturday=7, Sunday=1
)

# Group by week and calculate metrics
result = us_canada_notifications.PARTITION(name="weeks", by=week_start).CALCULATE(
    week_start=week_start,
    total_notifications=COUNT(Notifications),
    weekend_notifications=SUM(Notifications.is_weekend)
).ORDER_BY(week_start.ASC())

[DEBUG] [Q95] Code generated by gemini:
# Define the time boundaries for the last 3 weeks, excluding the current week.
# 'current_week_start_val' will be the exclusive end date (start of the current week).
# 'three_weeks_ago_start_val' will be the inclusive start date (start of the week, 3 weeks ago).
time_config = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    current_week_start_val=DATETIME('now', 'start of week'),
    three_weeks_ago_start_val=DATETIME('now', 'start of week', '-3 weeks')
)

# 1. Filter users to include only those from 'US' or 'Canada'.
# 2. Access their notifications.
# 3. Filter notifications to include only those created within the defined 3-week window.
# 4. For each of these notifications, calculate:
#    a. 'notification_week_start_ts': The timestamp representing the start of the week in which the notification was created. This will be used for grouping.
#    b. 'is_weekend_notification_num': A numeric flag, 1 if the notification was created on a Saturday or Sunday, 0 otherwise. This will be used for summing weekend notifications.
processed_notifications = time_config.Users.WHERE(
    ISIN(country, ("US", "Canada"))
).notifications.WHERE(
    (created_at >= three_weeks_ago_start_val) & (created_at < current_week_start_val)
).CALCULATE(
    notification_week_start_ts=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of week'),
    is_weekend_notification_num=IFF(ISIN(DAYNAME(created_at), ("Saturday", "Sunday")), 1, 0)
)

# 1. Partition the 'processed_notifications' by the 'notification_week_start_ts'.
#    This groups all notifications by the week they were sent.
# 2. For each week (each partition group):
#    a. Select the 'notification_week_start_ts' as 'week_output'.
#    b. Calculate 'total_notifications_output': Count all notifications within this week's group.
#    c. Calculate 'weekend_notifications_output': Sum the 'is_weekend_notification_num' flag for all notifications in this week's group. This effectively counts notifications sent on weekends.
# 3. Order the results by week in descending order, so the most recent of the three weeks appears first.
weekly_summary_report = processed_notifications.PARTITION(
    name="weekly_group", by=(notification_week_start_ts)
).CALCULATE(
    week_output=notification_week_start_ts,
    total_notifications_output=COUNT(processed_notifications),
    weekend_notifications_output=SUM(processed_notifications.is_weekend_notification_num)
).ORDER_BY(week_output.DESC())

result = weekly_summary_report

[DEBUG] [Q98] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter UserBalances for records updated in the past 7 days (inclusive)
# DATEDIFF("days", updated_at, 'now') gives the number of full days between updated_at and now.
# We want records from 7 days ago up to now, so the difference should be less than or equal to 7.
balances_in_last_week = Ewallet.UserBalances.WHERE(DATEDIFF("days", updated_at, DATETIME('now')) <= 7)

# Step 2: For each user, calculate their average balance (AMB) from these filtered records.
# We partition by user_id to group balance records by user.
# Then, for each user group, we calculate the average of their balances.
amb_per_user_calculation = balances_in_last_week.PARTITION(name="user_balances_group", by=(user_id)).CALCULATE(
    user_id=user_id,  # Include user_id from partition key
    user_amb_value=AVG(UserBalances.balance)  # Calculate average balance for this user's records in the group
)

# Step 3: Calculate the average of these per-user AMBs.
# This gives the final "average balance" requested, which is the average of individual user AMBs.
average_amb_result = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    average_balance=AVG(amb_per_user_calculation.user_amb_value)
)

# This contextless expression defines how to get the single, most recent balance record
# from a user's list of balances.
# 'balances' here will refer to the 'balances' sub-collection of a user.
# 'per="Users"' ensures ranking is done per individual user within their balances.

    RANKING(by=updated_at.DESC(), per="Users") == 1
).SINGULAR()

# Iterate through all users in the Ewallet.Users collection.
# For each user, calculate their uid and LUB.

    uid=uid,
    # Apply the 'get_latest_balance_record' expression to get the user's latest balance.
    # If 'get_latest_balance_record' is null (e.g., user has no balances),
    # DEFAULT_TO ensures 'lub' will be None.
    lub=DEFAULT_TO(get_latest_balance_record.balance, None)
)

[DEBUG] [Q102] Code generated by claude:
# Filter transactions for user senders in the last 2 months (excluding current month)
user_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", created_at, DATETIME('now')) >= 1) &
    (DATEDIFF("months", created_at, DATETIME('now')) <= 2)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month=DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'),
    sender_id=sender_id
)

# Group by month and calculate PMDAU (distinct senders per month)
result = user_transactions.PARTITION(name="monthly_groups", by=transaction_month).CALCULATE(
    month=transaction_month,
    pmdau=NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id)
).ORDER_BY(month.ASC())
[DEBUG] [Q102] DataFrame from claude is valid
INFO:httpx:HTTP Request: POST https://generativelanguage.googleapis.com/v1beta/models/gemini-2.5-pro-preview-05-06:generateContent "HTTP/1.1 200 OK"

[DEBUG] [Q102] Code generated by gemini:
# Define date boundaries for the last two months, excluding the current month
current_month_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')
two_months_ago_start_date = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-2 months')

# Step 1: Filter transactions by sender_type and the defined date range.
# Extract sender_id and transform created_at into month and day representations for grouping.
transactions_in_period = Transactions.WHERE(
    (sender_type == 0) &
    (created_at >= two_months_ago_start_date) &
    (created_at < current_month_start_date)
).CALCULATE(
    tx_sender_id = sender_id,
    year_month_key = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'), # e.g., 2023-10-01 00:00:00
    full_date_day_key = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of day')   # e.g., 2023-10-15 00:00:00
)

# Step 2: Calculate Daily Active Users (DAU).
# Group by month and day, then count distinct senders for each day.
daily_active_user_counts = transactions_in_period.PARTITION(
    name="daily_user_group", by=(year_month_key, full_date_day_key)
).CALCULATE(
    # These fields are the partition keys, brought into the calculation result
    calc_year_month = year_month_key,
    # calc_full_date_day = full_date_day_key, # Not strictly needed for the next aggregation step
    daily_active_users = NDISTINCT(Transactions.sender_id) # Count distinct senders within this day's transactions
)

# Step 3: Calculate Per Month Daily Active Users (PMDAU).
# Group the daily DAU counts by month and calculate the average DAU for each month.
pmdau_final_result = daily_active_user_counts.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_avg_group", by=(calc_year_month)
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month = calc_year_month, # The month for which PMDAU is calculated

    (YEAR(created_at) == YEAR(DATETIME('now', '-1 month'))) &
    (MONTH(created_at) == MONTH(DATETIME('now', '-1 month')))
)

result = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    total_transactions=COUNT(previous_month_transactions),
    successful_transactions=COUNT(previous_month_transactions.WHERE(
        (status == "completed") | (status == "success")
    )),
    str_rate=COUNT(previous_month_transactions.WHERE(
        (status == "completed") | (status == "success")
    )) * 100.0 / COUNT(previous_month_transactions)
)

# Define the start and end boundaries for the previous month dynamically.
# prev_month_start_boundary is the first moment of the previous month.
# curr_month_start_boundary is the first moment of the current month.
prev_month_start_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of month', '-1 months')
curr_month_start_boundary = DATETIME('now', 'start of month')

# Filter transactions that occurred strictly within the previous month.
transactions_in_prev_month = Transactions.WHERE(
    (created_at >= prev_month_start_boundary) & (created_at < curr_month_start_boundary)
)

# Count the number of successful transactions in the previous month.
# We assume a transaction is 'successful' if its status is 'completed'.
num_successful_tx_prev_month = COUNT(
    transactions_in_prev_month.WHERE(status == 'completed')
)

# Count the total number of transactions in the previous month.
total_tx_prev_month = COUNT(transactions_in_prev_month)

# Calculate STR (Success Transaction Rate) for the previous month.
# STR = (Number of successful transactions / Total number of transactions) * 100.
# If total_tx_prev_month is 0, STR is 0.0 to avoid division by zero.
calculated_str = IFF(
    total_tx_prev_month > 0,
    (num_successful_tx_prev_month * 100.0) / total_tx_prev_month,
    0.0
)

# Assign the calculated STR to a field in the Ewallet collection.
# The result will be a single row with the STR for the previous month.
wallet_str_previous_month = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    success_transaction_rate_previous_month = calculated_str
)

        (status == "completed") &

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only those made by active users in 2023,
# that were completed, and extract the transaction month and sender's UID.
filtered_user_transactions = Transactions.WHERE(
    (YEAR(created_at) == 2023) &
    (status == "completed") &
    (HAS(sending_user) == 1) &  # Ensures the sender is a user
    (sending_user.status == "active") # Ensures the sending user is active
).CALCULATE(
    transaction_month_date = DATETIME(created_at, 'start of month'), # Get the first day of the transaction month
    user_sender_uid = sending_user.uid # Get the UID of the sending user
)

# Step 2: Partition the filtered transactions by the transaction month.
# Then, for each month, calculate the distinct count of active users who sent money.
# Order the results by month in ascending order.
monthly_active_senders = filtered_user_transactions.PARTITION(
    name="monthly_transactions_group", by=(transaction_month_date)
).CALCULATE(
    month_as_date = transaction_month_date, # Output the month (as the first day of the month)
    num_active_users = NDISTINCT(user_sender_uid) # Count distinct active users for that month
).ORDER_BY(month_as_date.ASC())

result = monthly_active_senders

# Step 1: Filter transactions to include only those sent by users (sender_type = 0)
# and calculate necessary details: sender's country, sender's UID, and transaction amount.
# The 'sending_user' relationship links a transaction to the Users collection.
transaction_details_for_senders = Transactions.WHERE(sender_type == 0).CALCULATE(
    sender_country_field = sending_user.country,
    sender_user_id_field = sending_user.uid,
    transaction_amount_field = amount
)

# Step 2: Partition the transaction details by the sender's country.
# For each country, aggregate the number of distinct sending users and the sum of transaction amounts.
country_level_summary = transaction_details_for_senders.PARTITION(name="country_group", by=(sender_country_field)).CALCULATE(
    country_name_output = sender_country_field,
    distinct_sending_users_count = NDISTINCT(transaction_details_for_senders.sender_user_id_field),
    total_transaction_amount_for_country = SUM(transaction_details_for_senders.transaction_amount_field)
)

# Step 3: Select the top 5 countries based on the total transaction amount, in descending order.
top_5_countries_by_total_amount = country_level_summary.TOP_K(5, by=total_transaction_amount_for_country.DESC())

# Step 4: Prepare the final result with the requested column names.
result = top_5_countries_by_total_amount.CALCULATE(
    country = country_name_output,
    number_of_distinct_users_who_sent = distinct_sending_users_count,
    total_transaction_amount = total_transaction_amount_for_country
)

[DEBUG] [Q120] Code generated by gemini:
# Step 1: Filter merchants to find those that are active and have 'retail' in their category.
# We use LOWER() on the category field to ensure the 'contains' check is case-insensitive.
active_retail_merchants = Merchants.WHERE(
    (status == 'active') & CONTAINS(LOWER(category), 'retail')
)

# Step 2: Access the balances of these filtered merchants and further filter for balances updated today.
# DATE(updated_at) extracts the date part of the timestamp.
# DATE(DATETIME('now')) provides the current date.
todays_balances_for_active_retail_merchants = active_retail_merchants.balances.WHERE(
    DATE(updated_at) == DATE(DATETIME('now'))
)

# Step 3: Calculate the median of the 'balance' from the filtered balances.
# This will result in a single value: the median balance for the specified group of merchants today.
median_balance_output = Ewallet.CALCULATE(
    median_merchant_balance_today = MEDIAN(todays_balances_for_active_retail_merchants.balance)
)

average_session_duration_by_device_type = UserSessions.CALCULATE(
    # Calculate the duration of each session in seconds
    # device_type is an existing field in UserSessions and will be used for partitioning
    # session_start_ts and session_end_ts are existing fields
    session_duration_in_seconds=DATEDIFF("seconds", session_start_ts, session_end_ts)

    avg_session_duration_seconds=AVG(UserSessions.session_duration_in_seconds) # Aggregate the calculated duration

merchants_with_coupons = Merchants.WHERE(HAS(coupons) == 1)

merchants_with_earliest_coupon_info = merchants_with_coupons.CALCULATE(
    merchant_mid = mid,
    merchant_reg_date = created_at,
    # Get the singular earliest coupon by ordering by start_date and taking the first one.
    earliest_coupon = coupons.TOP_K(1, by=start_date.ASC()).SINGULAR()
)

# Filter merchants where the earliest coupon's start date is within one year of registration.
# This means the coupon start date is on or after the registration date,
# and on or before one year from the registration date.
result = merchants_with_earliest_coupon_info.WHERE(
    (earliest_coupon.start_date >= merchant_reg_date) &
    (earliest_coupon.start_date <= DATETIME(merchant_reg_date, '1 year'))
).CALCULATE(
    merchant_id = merchant_mid,
    registration_date = merchant_reg_date,
    earliest_coupon_id = earliest_coupon.cid,
    earliest_coupon_start_date = earliest_coupon.start_date
)
